Compare homogeneous and heterogeneous mixtures

CL-0074 · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · Virtual practical

Aim

Compare phase evidence and finite-band composition in a prepared solution, settling suspension, separating immiscible liquid reference and ideal stable density-matched droplet dispersion.

Principle and apparatus

Four virtual equal-volume columns, clock, phase view, schematic microscope and nonwithdrawal virtual band scanner. A homogeneous solution is one prepared phase. Distinct solid/liquid or immiscible-liquid domains remain heterogeneous even when bulk samples initially match. Three matching bands cannot establish molecular-scale homogeneity; use known phase evidence as well. The scanner integrates each finite band without extracting liquid. Its readings are exact reference quantities,not a calibrated chemical assay.

Procedure

  1. Record vessel volume, reference concentration or dispersed-phase volume fraction, chosen front speed and finite scan-band size. These references do not all contain equal B mass.
  2. Record all three bands before separation. Compare uniform bulk distribution with known phase identities.
  3. Run or pause the animated sequence. Observe descending suspension front, growing wet porous sediment and rising immiscible liquid. Record top,middle,bottom bands at the same physical time.
  4. Include a band crossing a boundary. Calculate exact overlap lengths,phase volumes,masses,mass fraction and concentration. Do not replace a finite band with a point reading.
  5. Compare the stable droplet reference with the single-phase solution. Uniform bulk samples alone are not proof that only one phase exists.
  6. Check integrated vessel volume and whole-vessel A/B mass. Virtual scans remove nothing; a real withdrawn aliquot would change the vessel inventory.
  7. Write a result and conclusion using phase evidence and concentration differences. State limitations and distinguish the mean of three bands from the whole-vessel average.

Prediction and plan

<mixture> Record finite top,middle,bottom bands at matched times; use phase evidence as well as concentration.

Formula substitution and worked calculations

Trial 1: Prepared single-phase solution · bottom

  1. Liquid height

    H = V/A

    Substitution: 100.000000/12

    Result: 8.333333 cm

  2. Chosen initial dispersed fraction

    φ = VB,0/V

    Substitution: Not used for the prepared single-phase solution

    Result: Not applicable: dissolved solute has no separately assigned phase volume

  3. Predicted separation time

    No separating front

    Substitution: No separating front in the selected reference

    Result: Not applicable: no separating front in this reference

  4. Bounded front travel

    d = 0

    Substitution: No separating front

    Result: 0 cm

  5. Region 1: total envelope volume

    Vr = A(hr,top−hr,bottom)

    Substitution: 12×(8.333333−0)

    Result: 100.000000 mL

  6. Integrated whole-vessel volume

    V = ΣVr

    Substitution: 100.000000

    Result: 100.000000 mL

  7. Whole-vessel B phase volume

    VB = ΣφrVr

    Substitution: Dissolved solute is not assigned a separate physical phase volume

    Result: Not applicable: dissolved solute has no separately assigned phase volume

  8. Whole-vessel B mass

    mB = CBV/1000

    Substitution: 50.000000×100.000000/1000

    Result: 5.000000 g

  9. Whole-vessel carrier-A mass

    mA = ρsolutionV−mB

    Substitution: 1.05×100.000000−5.000000

    Result: 100.000000 g

  10. Whole-vessel total mass

    m = mA+mB

    Substitution: 100.000000+5.000000

    Result: 105.000000 g

  11. Whole-vessel B concentration

    CB,global =1000mB/V

    Substitution: 1000×5.000000/100.000000

    Result: 50.000000 g/L

  12. Whole-vessel B mass fraction

    wB = mB/(mA+mB)

    Substitution: 5.000000/(100.000000+5.000000)

    Result: 0.047619 mass fraction

  13. Band centre

    yc = fcentreH

    Substitution: 0.15×8.333333

    Result: 1.250000 cm

  14. Band thickness

    Δy = fbandH

    Substitution: 0.100000×8.333333

    Result: 0.833333 cm

  15. Band lower boundary

    ylow = yc−Δy/2

    Substitution: 1.250000−0.833333/2

    Result: 0.833333 cm

  16. Band upper boundary

    yhigh = yc+Δy/2

    Substitution: 1.250000+0.833333/2

    Result: 1.666667 cm

  17. Finite band volume

    Vband = A(yhigh−ylow)

    Substitution: 12×(1.666667−0.833333)

    Result: 10.000000 mL; virtual nonwithdrawal scan

  18. Band overlap with region 1

    Δyr = max(0,min(yhigh,hr,top)−max(ylow,hr,bottom))

    Substitution: max(0,min(1.666667,8.333333)−max(0.833333,0))

    Result: 0.833333 cm

  19. Band B phase volume

    VB,band = AΣφrΔyr

    Substitution: Dissolved solute is not a separate phase

    Result: Not applicable: dissolved solute has no separately assigned phase volume

  20. Band carrier-A phase volume

    VA,band = Vband−VB,band

    Substitution: Carrier and solute partial phase volumes are not independently assigned

    Result: Not applicable: dissolved solute has no separately assigned phase volume

  21. Band B mass

    mB,band = CBVband/1000

    Substitution: 50.000000×10.000000/1000

    Result: 0.500000 g

  22. Band carrier-A mass

    mA,band = ρsolutionVband−mB,band

    Substitution: 1.05×10.000000−0.500000

    Result: 10.000000 g

  23. Band total mass

    mband = mA,band+mB,band

    Substitution: 10.000000+0.500000

    Result: 10.500000 g

  24. Band B mass fraction

    wB,band = mB,band/mband

    Substitution: 0.500000/10.500000

    Result: 0.047619 mass fraction,not volume fraction

  25. Band B concentration

    CB,band =1000mB,band/Vband

    Substitution: 1000×0.500000/10.000000

    Result: 50.000000 g/L

  26. bottom: B concentration

    CB =1000mB/Vband

    Substitution: 1000×0.500000/10.000000

    Result: 50.000000 g/L

  27. middle: B concentration

    CB =1000mB/Vband

    Substitution: 1000×0.500000/10.000000

    Result: 50.000000 g/L

  28. top: B concentration

    CB =1000mB/Vband

    Substitution: 1000×0.500000/10.000000

    Result: 50.000000 g/L

  29. Mean of three sampled bands

    Cmean = (Cbottom+Cmiddle+Ctop)/3

    Substitution: (50.000000+50.000000+50.000000)/3

    Result: 50.000000 g/L; not necessarily the whole-vessel mean

  30. Three-band concentration range

    ΔC = Cmax−Cmin

    Substitution: 50.000000−50.000000

    Result: 0 g/L

  31. Three-band population standard deviation

    σ = sqrt[Σ(Ci−Cmean)²/3]

    Substitution: sqrt[((50.000000−50.000000)²+(50.000000−50.000000)²+(50.000000−50.000000)²)/3]

    Result: 0 g/L; three deterministic bands,not a confidence interval

  32. Phase-based classification

    Known single solution phase → homogeneous; distinct dispersed phases → heterogeneous

    Substitution: Whole vessel has 1 known phases; three-band range=0g/L

    Result: homogeneous

Trial 2: Prepared single-phase solution · middle

  1. Liquid height

    H = V/A

    Substitution: 100.000000/12

    Result: 8.333333 cm

  2. Chosen initial dispersed fraction

    φ = VB,0/V

    Substitution: Not used for the prepared single-phase solution

    Result: Not applicable: dissolved solute has no separately assigned phase volume

  3. Predicted separation time

    No separating front

    Substitution: No separating front in the selected reference

    Result: Not applicable: no separating front in this reference

  4. Bounded front travel

    d = 0

Result and conclusion

A solution is one prepared phase. Settling solids and immiscible liquids retain distinct phases. The stable droplet reference remains heterogeneous despite matching bulk samples. A finite band crossing an interface must be integrated; scans remove no material.

Report classification with known phases,finite-band concentration differences and conserved constituent masses. A stable dispersion can remain heterogeneous despite matching bulk scans. Sediment is a wet porous envelope,not pure dry solid. Mass fraction and phase-volume fraction are different. Three deterministic samples do not constitute a complete unknown-sample purity test.

Precautions and limitations

Prepared generic references,not recipes for specific chemicals. Fixed column area12cm² and chosen densities: carrier1,solid2.65,upper immiscible liquid0.8,stable droplets1 and prepared solution1.05g/mL. Constant sharp-front speeds are chosen phenomenological parameters,not predictions from particle radius,viscosity or calibrated hydrodynamics. Wet sediment contains60%solid by envelope volume. Ideal stable density-matched droplets do not settle/coalesce; no surface chemistry or stability-lifetime prediction. Solution concentration and total measured reference density are specified independently; no solute partial-volume or dissolution kinetics solver. No dissolution/chemical reaction/temperature/optical scattering/assay noise. Microscopic phase domains and directional markers are explanatory glyphs,not extra quantitative inventory or resolved particles. Nonwithdrawal scans; real pipetting changes material balance. Editorial class placement; detailed finite-volume equations are teacher extensions.
